Browse All / Neurocutaneous Melanocytosis

Neurocutaneous Melanocytosis

MONDO:0009578 Pathograph 24 Show in embeddings browser congenital melanocytic naevus syndrome

A mosaic disorder in which one post-zygotic NRAS mutation, arising in a melanoblast precursor before the neural crest has finished migrating, leaves melanocytic lesions in two places at once: congenital melanocytic naevi in the skin, and melanocytosis of the leptomeninges and brain parenchyma. The same codon 61 mutation is found in the skin and in the central nervous system lesions of one patient and is absent from their blood, which is what makes this one disease rather than two. Most central nervous system involvement is benign and much of it is silent, but it can obstruct cerebrospinal fluid flow, cause epilepsy, and transform to melanoma; symptomatic disease carries a poor prognosis. Non-melanocytic malformations - Dandy-Walker, arachnoid cysts, cortical dysplasia - occur in the same patients, and the non-melanocytic central nervous system samples that were sequenced in one series all carried the mutation. That series does not say which lesions those samples came from, so the mutation is established for the class and not for any named malformation.

Inheritance

1
Somatic mosaicism HP:0001442
The causal NRAS mutation is post-zygotic. It is present in affected skin and in affected central nervous system tissue and absent from blood and unaffected tissue, so the disease exists only as a mosaic. No cited source describes a constitutional form.
typified by somatic mosaicism
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:23392294 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Oncogenic missense mutations in codon 61 of NRAS were found in affected neurological and cutaneous tissues of 12 out of 15 patients, but were absent from unaffected tissues and blood, consistent with NRAS mutation mosaicism."
The mosaicism, demonstrated the way mosaicism has to be: the mutation is in the affected tissue on both sides of the disease and in neither the blood nor the unaffected tissue of the same patient, in twelve of fifteen.
PMID:33048248 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Pathogenically, single postzygotic mutations in the NRAS (neuroblastoma RAS viral oncogene homologue; MIM # 164790; at 1p13.2) proto-oncogene explain the occurrence of single/multiple CMNs and melanocytic and non-melanocytic nervous system lesions in NCM: these disrupt the RAS/ERK/mTOR/PI3K/akt pathways."
A review stating the same thing as the field's settled account, and adding what this entry's pathophysiology turns on: the one post-zygotic mutation explains the non-melanocytic nervous system lesions as well as the melanocytic ones.

Discussions and Knowledge Gaps

3
Does screening every infant with more than one congenital melanocytic naevus for central nervous system involvement change what happens to them?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN ncm_screening_mri_value
Attached to
diagnosis#Screening brain and spine magnetic resonance imaging progression#Asymptomatic central nervous system involvement
Imaging finds an abnormality in a fifth to a third of the children scanned in the two screening series the diagnosis row names, depending on the series and on what counts as an abnormality. No cited source shows that finding it early changes the course, and no treatment has been shown to change the course. What the cited sources do establish is weaker and still useful: an abnormal scan is the most significant predictor of abnormal neurodevelopment, seizures and the need for neurosurgery in the largest screened cohort, and that cohort's authors conclude a single screening scan is a clinically relevant strategy. Prediction is not benefit, which is why this stays a gap rather than a recommendation. The study that set out to answer this names the question in its own objective. Against that, the entry's own progression rows record that patients with the symptomatic form typically die within three years of symptom onset, so the argument for finding it early is not nothing - it is untested. The entry's four prevalence records among people with congenital melanocytic naevi span 4.8% to 31.6%, partly because they do not all count the same thing - one is asymptomatic involvement only - so the size of the screened-positive group is not settled either.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:25966033 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Abnormal MRI was the most significant predictor of all outcome measures."
What the screened cohort established, which is prediction rather than benefit: the scan predicts abnormal neurodevelopment, seizures and the need for neurosurgery. The gap this discussion records is the step from predicting to changing.
PMID:16310055 SUPPORT DIRECT BACKGROUND Human clinical
"Recent retrospective studies using magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) to screen for neurocutaneous melanocytosis (NCM) among neurologically asymptomatic children with large congenital melanocytic nevi (LCMN) report high prevalence (23-30%) of asymptomatic NCM. We sought to determine prevalence of..."
The size of the asymptomatic group as the literature had it, and a second study setting out to measure it - which then reported 4.8%. The gap between those figures is part of why the question stays open.
Does inhibiting the pathway the NRAS mutation activates change the course of the neural disease?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN ncm_targeted_therapy_untested
Attached to
treatments#MEK inhibition treatments#PI3K and mTOR pathway inhibition
The pathway is known, the drugs exist, and the evidence stops at case reports, one centre's series and a registry study, none with a controlled comparison. The series' four children, three of them with congenital naevi, had melanoma of the central nervous system; each improved on trametinib for one to nine months and then progressed through treatment and died, and in the registry study's cohort of 27 children with melanoma of the central nervous system in this disease, which adds published cases, none survived a year, including the nine given trametinib among other treatments. Of the single patients, one infant's leptomeningeal thickening fell rapidly on trametinib and he was developmentally normal at 53 months, but he received azacitidine at the same time. Another child took trametinib for seven months and died of malignant transformation of his leptomeningeal disease. A 13-year-old received a MEK inhibitor and died five days later, too soon for an effect, though his tissue showed reduced pERK afterwards. A teenager with parenchymal melanosis seen on imaging had no further decline at one-year follow-up, four to five months after trametinib was added to levetiracetam. A child with primary diffuse leptomeningeal melanomatosis, an aggressive cancer biologically closely related to this disease, received trametinib and everolimus followed by two immunotherapies with intraventricular chemotherapy, improved temporarily and survived seven months, so nothing in that course can be attributed to the MEK inhibitor either. MEK inhibition has relieved the symptoms of melanoma for a time; whether it changes the course of the benign neural lesion has not been tested in any comparison the cited reports describe. The cell-culture comparisons point at the PI3K/mTOR arm rather than the MEK arm, and the compound that performed best there has never been given to a patient in the cited literature. A phase II trial of mirdametinib for large and giant naevi is registered (NCT07891286) but not yet recruiting; its primary endpoint is the skin naevus, and it admits people with neurocutaneous melanocytosis only if that is asymptomatic; the disease is rare enough that a trial of the neural disease would have to be international.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:24713450 SUPPORT DIRECT BACKGROUND Human clinical
"Despite surgery, which is often not radical, few other treatment options exist."
The state of treatment stated plainly by one of the groups trying to change it.
PMID:24713450 SUPPORT DIRECT Human clinical
"Further studies are needed to determine whether MEK inhibitors can effectively target NRAS-mutated symptomatic NCM, a rare but potentially fatal disease."
The gap named by the authors of the one patient report that measured a pathway effect. No quote_role is recorded: this is a single-patient report's own call for future work, which is neither its finding nor somebody else's fact.
The mouse model of this disease carries NRAS G12D while every mutation-positive patient in the series that established the lesion - twelve of fifteen - carries a codon 61 substitution. Does the model's central nervous system melanoma arise the way the human one does?
HUMAN MODEL MISMATCH OPEN ncm_mouse_allele_mismatch
Attached to
pathophysiology#Post-zygotic NRAS Codon 61 Mutation animal_models#Embryonic melanocyte NRAS(G12D) mouse
The model is the main non-human evidence for this entry's lethal arm and it differs from the human disease in two ways that are not incidental. Its allele is at a different codon, and codon 61 is where the mutation sits in every mutation-positive patient of the series that established the lesion. And it is a transgenic rather than a mosaic, so the mutant cells are not surrounded by wild-type ones - which is the architecture the word mosaic in this disease's name refers to. The model's cutaneous lesions are reported as resembling blue naevi rather than congenital melanocytic naevi, which is consistent with the mismatch mattering rather than not. What the model does reproduce, early-onset central nervous system melanoma, is the result that made it worth building.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:23303902 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"NRAS is not a common driver oncogene of primary melanoma of the CNS in adults, but we report two cases of primary melanoma of the CNS in children, both of which carried oncogenic mutations in NRAS."
The human side the model is held against: two children, in whom the paper does not say which codon. INDIRECT because the match between model and patients is what is at issue and this sentence does not close it.
⚙

Pathophysiology

7
Post-zygotic NRAS Codon 61 Mutation
One activating missense mutation at codon 61 of NRAS, arising after fertilisation in a cell of the melanoblast lineage. Codon 61 is the hotspot: ten of twelve mutation-positive patients in the series that established this carried c.181C>A p.Q61K and two c.182A>G p.Q61R.

Downstream
Mosaic Melanocytic Clone Spanning Skin and Neuraxis A mutation in one cell becomes a disease in two organs only if that cell is an ancestor of both populations. That is the step this edge asserts, and the evidence for it is that the same mutation is in both and in neither the blood nor the unaffected tissue of the same patient.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:23392294 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"In 10 patients, the mutation was consistently c.181C>A, p.Q61K, and in 2 patients c.182A>G, p.Q61R."
The two alleles and their split across twelve mutation-positive patients, which is the source of the variant detail on this node.
PMID:33048248 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Pathogenically, single postzygotic mutations in the NRAS (neuroblastoma RAS viral oncogene homologue; MIM # 164790; at 1p13.2) proto-oncogene explain the occurrence of single/multiple CMNs and melanocytic and non-melanocytic nervous system lesions in NCM: these disrupt the RAS/ERK/mTOR/PI3K/akt pathways."
A review stating the lesion and the pathways it acts through. The molecular function is graded GAIN_OF_FUNCTION and the process INCREASED for the usual reason: a RAS codon 61 substitution takes the switch outside its normal regulatory control, and what is observed downstream is a pathway running above normal.
Mosaic Melanocytic Clone Spanning Skin and Neuraxis
The mutant clone, distributed by neural crest migration. Its descendants reach the skin and the leptomeninges, which is why the two halves of this disease are one disease. The clone is not confined to melanocytes: the non-melanocytic central nervous system lesions of these patients carry the same mutation.

Experimental models
Patient-derived nevomelanocytic spheroids PRIMARY CELL CULTURE The cells are the clone, taken from both compartments of the same patients, and their identity was confirmed by the mutation rather than assumed.
Animal models
Embryonic melanocyte NRAS(G12D) mouse Mouse partially recapitulates Reproduces the embryonic timing and the two-compartment distribution: congenital melanocytic lesions in the skin and melanocytic disease in the central nervous system from one driver.
Downstream
Congenital Melanocytic Naevi The cutaneous half: the clone's descendants that reach the skin.
Leptomeningeal and Parenchymal Melanocytosis The neural half: the clone's descendants that stay with the neuraxis. Where they settle is not random - the amygdala is the commonest site on screening imaging.
Non-Melanocytic Central Nervous System Malformation The malformations carry the mutation too, which is why they belong in this entry's chain rather than beside it as coincidence. No cited source shows how a RAS-activated clone produces a Dandy-Walker malformation or an arachnoid cyst, so the edge is drawn through unknown intermediates.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:1869648 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Neurocutaneous melanosis is a rare congenital syndrome characterized by the presence of large or multiple congenital melanocytic nevi and benign or malignant pigment cell tumors of the leptomeninges."
The definition the field still uses, from the 1991 review of 39 cases that proposed the revised criteria. It names the two lesions this node's clone produces, which is the claim the node makes; it says nothing about when the mutation arose, so it does not sit on the inheritance block.
PMID:23392294 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"All 11 non-melanocytic and melanocytic CNS samples from 5 patients were mutation positive, despite NRAS rarely being reported as mutated in CNS tumors."
Every central nervous system sample tested carried the mutation, melanocytic and non-melanocytic alike. The second clause matters as much as the first: NRAS is not a common driver in central nervous system tumours, so finding it in all eleven is evidence of a shared clone rather than of a recurrent somatic event.
PMID:23303902 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"When NRAS(G12D) was expressed in the melanocytes of developing embryos, it induced melanocyte proliferation and congenital melanocytic lesions reminiscent of human blue nevi but did not induce cutaneous melanoma."
The clone's behaviour modelled in mouse embryos, and the model's own limit. Expressing oncogenic NRAS in embryonic melanocytes produced congenital melanocytic lesions and proliferation, which is this node; it did not produce cutaneous melanoma, and the allele is G12D rather than the codon 61 one patients carry.
Congenital Melanocytic Naevi
The cutaneous lesions, present at birth, large or multiple or both, often with overlying hypertrichosis and with smaller satellite naevi. Their size, distribution, satellite count and surface features are what predict the neural half, which is why they are a mechanism node here and not only a phenotype.

Downstream
Hypertrichosis Hair overlying the naevus, which a review names inside its definition of the syndrome rather than as an associated finding.
Melanocytic naevi of the skin The lesion as the clinical finding a parent and a clinician see at birth.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37777590 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Distribution and size of CMN, number of satellite nevi, rugosity and nodules were strongly associated with the existence of NCM, and these findings may guide a future registry study with a large cohort of CMN patients."
The features of the cutaneous lesion that predict the neural one. The authors' own hedge is kept: this is an association in 38 infants, offered as a basis for a registry study rather than as a rule.
Leptomeningeal and Parenchymal Melanocytosis
Melanocytes where there should be almost none: infiltrating the leptomeninges and deposited in the brain parenchyma. In a screening series of 38 Japanese infants with congenital melanocytic naevi, about a third had it on brain MRI; that series' abstract gives no symptom data, and no cited source, as cached, establishes what proportion of those found on screening go on to neurological disease. It is the lesion every consequence below depends on.
melanocyte CL:0000148 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves melanocyte (CL:0000148). CL:0000148 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
leptomeninx UBERON:0000391 Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology (UBERON) Relation: this pathophysiological event occurs in this anatomical location This pathophysiological event occurs in leptomeninx (UBERON:0000391). UBERON:0001876 is an anatomical location from the Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology.
Downstream
Obstruction of Cerebrospinal Fluid Flow Melanocytes infiltrating the leptomeninges obstruct cerebrospinal fluid flow. No cited source measures the obstruction; what they report is hydrocephalus in patients with the lesion, which is why the edge is drawn through unknown intermediates.
Cranial nerve palsy Melanocytic infiltration of the leptomeninges is where the cranial nerves run, and cranial nerve palsy is on the review's list of this disease's neurological manifestations. No cited source shows the infiltrate compressing a nerve, so the edge is drawn through unknown intermediates and could as well run through the raised pressure node.
Global developmental delay Profound delay occurs in a minority of children with the lesion and most have none or only mild delay, so this edge asserts a possible consequence and not a usual one. What separates them is partly established: in the largest screened cohort an abnormal scan was the most significant predictor of abnormal neurodevelopment, and the cohort split into intraparenchymal melanosis alone, which was not associated with malignancy or death even when symptomatic, and all other pathology, which was not so benign.
Seizures Epilepsy is the commonest neurological manifestation, and in the pooled neurosurgical literature the commonest symptom of any kind. How the melanocytic deposits produce it is not established by any cited source; focal cortical dysplasia in the same patients is one route and the deposits themselves another.
Malignant Transformation to Leptomeningeal Melanoma The melanocytosis is the substrate melanoma arises in. A second genetic event is implicated rather than shown: loss of heterozygosity accompanied the onset of melanoma in two patients of the series that established the NRAS lesion, which the authors read as a multistep progression.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:37777590 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"NCM was diagnosed on brain MRI in 31.6% of the 38 patients with CMN and in 25.0% of patients with no prior examination or treatment."
How common the lesion was among these infants, all of whom were imaged: about a third. Read the denominator - 38 Japanese infants starting treatment for their naevi, not an unselected population.
PMID:22469364 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"All patients had neuroimaging: six had findings suggestive of diffuse leptomeningeal melanocytosis; seven had multifocal melanocytic deposits; and one patient had normal neuroimaging but focal seizures."
The two radiological forms this node covers, in fourteen children referred to a neuro-oncology service - and the patient with seizures and a normal scan, which is why imaging is not the definition of the lesion.
Obstruction of Cerebrospinal Fluid Flow
Thickened, infiltrated leptomeninges impede cerebrospinal fluid circulation and absorption. This is the arm that brings patients to neurosurgery: cerebrospinal fluid shunting is the commonest operation performed in this disease.
leptomeninx UBERON:0000391 Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology (UBERON) Relation: this pathophysiological event occurs in this anatomical location This pathophysiological event occurs in leptomeninx (UBERON:0000391). UBERON:0000391 is an anatomical location from the Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology.
Downstream
Hydrocephalus The obstruction expressed as ventricular enlargement.
Increased intracranial pressure The pressure the obstruction raises. In 27 pooled cases of primary diffuse leptomeningeal melanomatosis, a cancer the review calls biologically closely related to this disease, it was present in two thirds, and the review names the non-specific presentation, with unrecognised raised pressure as its example, as a frequent reason for delayed diagnosis.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35535823 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"The most common surgical technique was CSF shunting (43%, 24/56 operations), with tethered cord release (4%, 2/56 operations) being the least frequently performed."
What is done about this node, which is also the measure of how often it matters: shunting is nearly half of all operations across 74 pooled patients.
Malignant Transformation to Leptomeningeal Melanoma
Melanoma arising within the leptomeningeal lesion. In a referral series of 14 patients, four of the six who died had leptomeningeal melanoma. In children with melanoma of the central nervous system in this disease, a German registry study with published cases added reports a median survival of 0.4 years; in 27 pooled cases of primary diffuse leptomeningeal melanomatosis, a related cancer, median overall survival is four months from diagnosis.

Animal models
Embryonic melanocyte NRAS(G12D) mouse Mouse partially recapitulates The model's unexpected result and the reason it is cited here: early-onset primary melanoma of the central nervous system, which is the human disease's lethal arm.
Downstream
Melanoma The malignancy as the clinical event.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:22469364 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Of the six patients who died, all had diffuse leptomeningeal melanocytic deposits and four had leptomeningeal melanoma."
In fourteen patients at one neuro-oncology service, all six who died had the leptomeningeal lesion and four had melanoma in it; the abstract gives no cause of death.
PMID:33921303 SUPPORT INDIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Mortality remains substantial despite diverse therapeutic approaches with a median overall survival of 4 months from diagnosis."
Survival in a related cancer: 27 pooled cases of primary diffuse leptomeningeal melanomatosis, which the review calls biologically closely related to this disease. It is not a survival figure for melanoma arising in neurocutaneous melanocytosis, and is indirect for that reason.
PMID:38225622 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"In the expanded cohort, no patient survived longer than 1 year after diagnosis despite multimodal therapy (including trametinib; n = 9), with a median survival of 0.4 years (range 0.1-0.9)."
A survival figure for melanoma of the central nervous system in this disease: 27 children from a German registry and published reports, none of whom survived a year. The published cases may include children counted in other series in this entry.
Non-Melanocytic Central Nervous System Malformation
Structural malformations in the same patients and carrying the same mutation: Dandy-Walker complex, arachnoid cysts, hemimegalencephaly, cortical dysplasia, Chiari malformations, syringomyelia. They are not melanocytic, which is why they are a separate node, and they are not coincidental, which is why they are in the chain.
brain UBERON:0000955 Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology (UBERON) Relation: this pathophysiological event occurs in this anatomical location This pathophysiological event occurs in brain (UBERON:0000955). UBERON:0000955 is an anatomical location from the Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology.
Downstream
Dandy-Walker malformation The commonest of them, at about a quarter of pooled published cases - the same frequency as hydrocephalus, with which it is not independent.
Arachnoid cyst Spinal arachnoid cysts were the commonest spinal abnormality in the referral series, in three of fourteen children.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:33048248 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Associated brain and spinal cord malformations include the Dandy-Walker malformation (DWM) complex, hemimegalencephaly, cortical dysplasia, arachnoid cysts, Chiari I and II malformations, syringomyelia, meningoceles, occult spinal dysraphism, and CNS lipoma/lipomatosis."
The list this node covers, from a review of the disease.

Genetic Associations

2
NRAS post-zygotic activating mutations
Gene: NRAS hgnc:7989 gene page HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is NRAS (hgnc:7989). hgnc:7989 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: CAUSATIVE variant_origin: SOMATIC
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:23392294 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"These results suggest that single postzygotic NRAS mutations are responsible for multiple CMN and associated neurological lesions in the majority of cases."
The authors' own statement of the gene-disease relationship, with their hedge: suggest, and in the majority of cases rather than all.
Variants (2)
p.Q61K
c.181C>A, the commoner of the two alleles, in ten of twelve mutation-positive patients.
p.Q61R
c.182A>G, in two of twelve.
BRAF
Gene: BRAF hgnc:1097 gene page HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is BRAF (hgnc:1097). hgnc:1097 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: CAUSATIVE variant_origin: SOMATIC
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:42384401 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"This case report describes sustained neurologic and cutaneous improvement following BRAF inhibition in a patient with BRAF V600E-variant neurocutaneous melanosis."
A BRAF V600E patient, and the consequence of being one: sustained improvement on a BRAF inhibitor. One patient, which is why this row claims the alternative lesion exists and nothing about how often.
PMID:25490715 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Neurocutaneous melanocytosis (NCM) affected 16 (24.2%) of 66 patients, with NRAS Q61 mutation in 12 (75.0%), and BRAF V600E in 2 (12.5%), P=0.009."
How often, from a prospective cohort of 66 congenital melanocytic naevus patients: of the 16 who had the neural lesion, 12 carried NRAS Q61 and 2 carried BRAF V600E. Two patients, which is the basis for recording BRAF as a causative alternative rather than only as a case report.
PMID:25490715 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"In conclusion, although NRAS Q61 mutations predominate, BRAF V600E mutation also affects patients with large/giant CMN (L/GCMN), and with NCM, a novel finding."
The authors' own statement of what is established and how new it is. The grade CAUSATIVE rests on this plus the treated case report, not on the case report alone.
💊

Medical Actions

5
Cerebrospinal fluid shunting
Action: cerebrospinal fluid diversionNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is cerebrospinal fluid diversion (NCIT:C93208). NCIT:C93208 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Cerebrospinal Fluid Diversion NCIT:C93208
Platform: Surgery
The commonest operation in this disease, done for the hydrocephalus rather than for the melanocytic lesion. It relieves the pressure and leaves everything upstream of it untouched.
Mechanism Target:
Obstruction of Cerebrospinal Fluid Flow — Diverting cerebrospinal fluid bypasses the obstructed pathway. It acts on no step above that node: the mutant clone, the melanocytosis and the risk of transformation are all unaddressed, which is why a shunted child is not a treated child.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:29948137 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Patient required placement of right-sided ventriculoperitoneal shunt to control hydrocephalus. The patient tolerated the procedure well and was discharged home with normal neurological function."
The operation acting on this node in one infant, with the outcome that makes the point: normal neurological function on discharge, and the same child died of malignant transformation of the leptomeningeal disease the shunt did not touch.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35535823 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"The most common surgical technique was CSF shunting (43%, 24/56 operations), with tethered cord release (4%, 2/56 operations) being the least frequently performed."
How often it is done, across 74 pooled published patients.
MEK inhibition
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: trametinib CHEBI:75998 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses trametinib (CHEBI:75998). CHEBI:75998 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest. binimetinib CHEBI:145371 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses binimetinib (CHEBI:145371). CHEBI:145371 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Blocking the pathway the mutation activates. The clinical evidence is case-level: single case reports, one centre's series of four children with NRAS-mutated melanoma of the central nervous system, and a registry study of melanoma of the central nervous system in this disease, none with a controlled comparison. In the series, where three of the four children had congenital naevi, every child improved in symptoms and signs on trametinib for between one and nine months, with no clinically significant side effects and, in one child, stable leptomeningeal disease on MRI at 56 and 139 days; every child then progressed through treatment and died. In the registry study, with published cases added, none of 27 children survived a year, including the nine given trametinib among other treatments, and 14 of the 16 with data had an NRAS alteration. The single patients did not go the same way. One infant's leptomeningeal thickening fell rapidly on trametinib with azacitidine and he was developmentally normal at 53 months. Another child took trametinib for seven months and eventually died of malignant transformation of his leptomeningeal disease. A 13-year-old died five days after binimetinib was started, too soon for a clinical effect, though his tissue showed lower pERK and Ki67 afterwards; the 2014 report calls the drug MEK162, which is binimetinib. A 13-year-old girl with melanosis of the amygdala and medial temporal lobes, seen only on imaging, had no further neurological decline at one-year follow-up, four to five months after trametinib was added to levetiracetam; within about two months of starting trametinib she had a breakthrough seizure-like episode and her levetiracetam dose was raised. Her skin changed in both directions: by her ten-month follow-up, when she had completed 10 weeks of trametinib, the naevus on her forehead had shrunk from 3.1 to 2.7 cm and the borders of her largest naevus looked lighter, while some of her other naevi were reportedly darker. A child with primary diffuse leptomeningeal melanomatosis, an aggressive cancer biologically closely related to this disease, received trametinib and everolimus, followed by nivolumab and ipilimumab with intraventricular chemotherapy; the child improved temporarily and survived seven months, and no part of that result can be attributed to the MEK inhibitor alone.
Mechanism Target:
Post-zygotic NRAS Codon 61 Mutation — The drug cannot undo the mutation; it acts on the pathway the mutation holds open, which this node binds as increased Ras protein signal transduction. Recorded against this node rather than a downstream one because that is where the target sits.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:24713450 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"We observed lower MIB-1 expression and lower pERK expression in the post-treatment samples compared to pre-treatment, suggesting a potential effect of MEK inhibiting therapy."
The pathway readout in one patient's own tumour before and after the drug, with the authors' hedge: suggesting a potential effect. The same patient died five days into treatment, so this is a molecular observation and not a clinical result.
Show evidence (15 references)
PMID:28253523 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"All four had an improvement in symptoms and objectively in signs. These varied from mild improvement for 1 month, to a sustained symptom-free period of 9 months in one case."
The series' result in four children with NRAS-mutated melanoma of the central nervous system, three of them with congenital naevi by the series' own table: a temporary improvement in every child. Indirect for this disease as a whole, because the children had melanoma of the central nervous system and one had no naevi.
PMID:28253523 REFUTE INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"In all cases there was eventual disease progression through treatment, followed by rapid death after discontinuation."
The same four children later: the improvement did not last, and every child died. REFUTE against the claim that MEK inhibition changes the course of the disease; indirect for the same reasons as the item before.
PMID:28253523 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"There were no clinically-significant side effects."
Tolerability in the same four children, as the description reports it.

PI3K and mTOR pathway inhibition

Platform: Small molecule
The other arm of the pathway the mutation opens. In nevospheres grown from four patients' own spinal cord, brain and skin lesions, the dual PI3K/mTOR inhibitor GSK2126458 reduced viability further than any other inhibitor tested - below half. One cited patient received an mTOR inhibitor, everolimus, together with trametinib and followed by two immunotherapies with intraventricular chemotherapy, so nothing can be attributed to it. One centre reports that another mTOR inhibitor, sirolimus, had no measurable effect in three children with melanoma of the central nervous system.

The comparison between inhibitors, in cells carrying the patients' own mutation. IN_VITRO, and four patients' lesions.

The same drug against the clonogenic growth that is this disease's cellular behaviour, rather than against bulk viability. Still in cells.

Available only to the minority with a BRAF variant, which is why the genetic row for BRAF is not a taxonomic footnote; the trametinib series' authors call selective BRAF inhibitors contraindicated in NRAS-driven melanoma of the central nervous system. 🔬

Diagnosis

3
Screening brain and spine magnetic resonance imaging
The test that finds the neural half, and the one argued about. Two screening series find an abnormality in a fifth to a third of the children they scanned, depending on the series and on what counts as an abnormality: the lesion on brain MRI in 31.6% of Japanese infants starting treatment for congenital melanocytic naevi, and an abnormal scan of any kind in 21% of the children born with more than one naevus whom one centre scanned. The abstracts of those series do not say what proportion were symptom-free. Whether finding the lesion changes anything is the open question this entry records. The cited recommendations differ on whom to scan. One centre's criterion since 2008 is a single MRI for every child born with more than one congenital naevus, whatever the size or site of the largest; the Nevosurgery Network recommends MRI for a giant naevus over 40 cm projected adult size, more than 20 concomitant naevi, or neurological symptoms needing clarification. A normal scan does not remove the risk of melanoma: in an earlier report from the same group, on children seen from 1991 to 2007, two children whose scans were normal died of it.
magnetic resonance imaging of the neuraxis NCIT:C16809 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (5 references)
PMID:37777590 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Japanese pediatric patients with CMN under 1 year of age were included between January 2020 and November 2022, and all patients underwent brain MRI to check for NCM in this study."
The screening design: every infant imaged, which is what makes the 31.6% figure a prevalence in that group rather than a yield in the symptomatic.
PMID:25966033 SUPPORT DIRECT Human clinical
"For screening for congenital neurological lesions a single MRI in multiple CMN is a clinically relevant strategy."
The conclusion of the screened cohort (289 children met its criterion for a scan), which is as close to an answer as the cited literature comes. It says the scan is a relevant strategy, not that acting on it changes the course; the entry's discussion records that second question as the one still open.
PMID:25966033 SUPPORT DIRECT Human clinical
"Of 376 patients seen between 1991 and 2013, 289 fulfilled our criterion for a single screening CNS MRI, which since 2008 has been more than one CMN at birth, independent of size and site of the largest naevus."
The first policy: the centre's screening criterion, more than one congenital naevus at birth whatever the size or site of the largest.
+ 2 more references
Cerebrospinal fluid cytology and immunohistochemistry
Used with imaging to confirm leptomeningeal melanosis without a biopsy of the meninges. The 1991 review that proposed the diagnostic criteria names it and gadolinium-enhanced magnetic resonance imaging as the useful procedures, which is where this row comes from.
immunohistochemistry on cerebrospinal fluid NCIT:C23020 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:29948137 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"A presumptive diagnosis of NCM was made based on the MR characteristics, CSF cytology and clinical presentation."
The three things a diagnosis rests on in practice, and the word the authors use for the result: presumptive. One patient.
PMID:1869648 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Useful diagnostic procedures include cerebrospinal fluid cytology and magnetic resonance imaging with gadolinium contrast."
The two procedures named together by the review that set the criteria, which is a better basis for this row than the single case above it.
Mutation testing of lesional tissue
Blood will not carry the mutation, so the test has to be on lesional tissue - naevus, leptomeninges, or cultured cells from either. This is the practical consequence of mosaicism and the reason a negative blood test means nothing here. One cited source, on primary diffuse leptomeningeal melanomatosis, reports that the tumour's somatic mutation could be detected in cerebrospinal fluid and offers this as a potential biomarker, a route that would need no lesional biopsy.
No diagnosis_term is bound. The sibling rows carry NCIT clinical-action terms, and the nearest one available here names immunohistochemistry, which is the wrong kind of test for this row; binding it would assert a method this row is not about. The preferred term for the action is carried by the row name instead.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:26354928 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In vitro / ex vivo
"Cultured cell identity was confirmed by demonstration of the same NRAS mutation found in the original lesions and by immunophenotyping."
The test performed on lesional material rather than on blood, here to confirm that cultured cells came from the lesion. INDIRECT for the diagnostic claim: the paper's purpose is a cell model, not a diagnostic workflow.
PMID:33921303 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"We further show, for the first time, that this somatic mutation can be exploited for cerebrospinal fluid liquid biopsy detection, revealing a novel potential biomarker for diagnosis and monitoring of PDLMM."
The report's own finding in primary diffuse leptomeningeal melanomatosis: the tumour's somatic mutation could be detected in cerebrospinal fluid. Indirect for this disease, and offered as a potential biomarker rather than a validated test.
PMID:33921303 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In vitro / ex vivo
"Last, we use a unique patient derived PDLMM cell model to provide first insights into in vitro drug sensitivities."
A second patient-derived cell system in the cited literature, from a primary diffuse leptomeningeal melanomatosis report. Cited on this row because what both systems depend on is the claim this row makes: the material has to come from the lesion.
🩻

Imaging Findings

2
T1 hyperintensity of parenchymal melanosis
Bright signal on T1-weighted images, before any contrast is given, where melanin is likely deposited in the central nervous system. In the Japanese screening series of infants the lesions were seen most often in the amygdala, then the cerebellum, the brainstem and the cerebral hemispheres; the series' abstract gives no counts by site. A case report's background names the amygdala, pons and cerebellum as the commonest sites, says melanosis can also be seen elsewhere in the central nervous system, including the spinal cord and cauda equina, and calls the signal typically bilateral.
Modality: MRIMagnetic Resonance Imaging
Finding: T1 hyperintensity before contrast NCIT:C182328 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Location: central nervous system UBERON:0001017 Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology (UBERON)
Timing matters. In two neonates, aged 11 and 23 days, the melanosis was not hidden by myelination and showed without contrast; over the middle and later months of the first year it becomes harder to see, and contrast-enhanced T1-weighted sequences may be needed. A scan without these changes does not exclude the disease: the case report's background cites patients with normal MRI and melanin deposits found at histopathology, and a review describes patients with or without the typical changes. T2 signal is reported both ways: the case report found its lesions bright on T2 and FLAIR and sets this against reports of decreased T2 and FLAIR signal from melanin, so only the T1 appearance is recorded as characteristic.
Show evidence (10 references)
PMID:37777590 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"NCM lesions were most frequently seen in the amygdala, followed by the cerebellum, brainstem, and cerebral hemispheres."
The order of sites in a prospective screening series of 38 Japanese infants. An order only: the abstract gives no count by site.
PMID:33257354 SUPPORT DIRECT BACKGROUND Human clinical
"The amygdala, pons and cerebellum are most commonly involved but melanosis can be seen anywhere in the central nervous system (CNS), including the spinal cord and cauda equina."
The sites as the case report's background states them, citing earlier work; it adds the spinal cord and cauda equina.
PMID:33257354 SUPPORT DIRECT BACKGROUND Human clinical
"On MRI, T1 hyperintensity, likely from melanin deposition, can be seen at these sites and is typically bilateral."
The signal itself and its likely source, melanin, from the same background passage; "these sites" are those of the sentence before.
+ 7 more references
Leptomeningeal thickening with T1 hyperintensity
MRI may show melanosis of the leptomeninges as thickening around the brain and spinal cord with high signal on T1-weighted images. A 2024 imaging article separates this diffuse leptomeningeal form from focal melanosis of the brain parenchyma, the more prevalent, because the two carry different risks: focal melanosis is more likely to cause epileptic seizures, and diffuse melanosis is significantly more often associated with hydrocephalus or even cerebral melanoma.
Modality: MRIMagnetic Resonance Imaging Extent: DiffuseWidespread, continuous distribution
Finding: leptomeningeal thickening with T1 hyperintensity HP:0010651 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP) Location: leptomeninges UBERON:0000391 Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology (UBERON)
Bound to HP:0010651 Abnormal meningeal morphology, the narrowest HPO term that covers thickening of the leptomeninges around both the brain and the spinal cord. Its nearest child terms, as OLS listed them on 20 November 2026, name contrast enhancement (HP:0032070 Leptomeningeal enhancement), one layer (HP:0100700 Abnormal arachnoid mater morphology, HP:0100701 Abnormal pia mater) or the spinal meninges only (HP:0010303 Abnormal spinal meningeal morphology). OLS searches of HPO and NCIT on 20 November 2026 for "leptomeningeal thickening", "meningeal thickening" and "thickened meninges" returned no term for leptomeningeal thickening. NCIT's two leptomeningeal disease findings (NCIT:C162991 Classical Leptomeningeal Disease Finding, NCIT:C162992 Nodular Leptomeningeal Disease Finding) are defined as diffuse spread of neoplastic cells to the leptomeninges, which may show as linear or nodular enhancement on MRI, so they do not fit either.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:41474606 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"MRI might reveal meningeal thickening surrounding the brain and spinal cord, with characteristic high signal intensity on T1-weighted sequences and low signal intensity on T2-weighted sequences, indicating the presence of melanin and suggesting a diagnosis of NCM."
The appearance of the leptomeningeal form, from the literature review of a fetal case report.
PMID:39518313 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"When the CNS is involved, it is crucial to differentiate between focal melanosis of the brain parenchyma, which is more prevalent, and disseminated or diffuse melanosis of the leptomeninges. While focal melanosis is more likely to precipitate epileptic seizures, diffuse melanosis is..."
The distinction between the two forms and the different risks the article attaches to each.
📈

Progression

4
Congenital cutaneous lesion
The naevi are present at birth and are what prompts everything else. Nothing neurological is expected at this point. How many go on to develop symptoms is not settled by the cited sources and the entry does not assert a majority either way: in the registry series, 17 of the 26 patients reported to have the neural lesion also reported neurological symptoms, while the abstract of the imaging series that found the lesion in a third of infants gives no symptom data.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:16310055 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"Of 379 patients with LCMN, 26 reportedly had NCM, with 17 reporting neurologic symptoms."
The split between having the neural lesion and having symptoms, in a registry of 379 patients with large congenital melanocytic naevi. Self-reported, which is why the entry does not use these as rates.
Asymptomatic central nervous system involvement
Found only by imaging. One series that scanned every infant, 38 Japanese infants under one year treated for congenital melanocytic naevi, found the lesion on brain MRI in about a third, and in a quarter of those not previously examined or treated; its abstract does not say how many had symptoms. Whether this phase progresses is the question the entry's screening discussion records; the 14-patient referral series includes a child with focal seizures and a normal scan, so the two do not map onto each other.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37777590 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"NCM was diagnosed on brain MRI in 31.6% of the 38 patients with CMN and in 25.0% of patients with no prior examination or treatment."
How often brain MRI found the lesion in 38 Japanese infants treated for congenital melanocytic naevi, with the second figure for those not previously examined or treated. The abstract does not say how many had symptoms, so for this phase the figures are an upper bound for lesions seen on brain MRI.
Symptomatic neurological disease
Acute in infancy or, more often, later in childhood or adult life - though the 1991 review that set the criteria found most of its 39 cases presenting in the first two years. Once symptoms appear the outlook changes sharply: a systematic review states in its framing that patients typically die within three years of symptom onset, and the 1991 review found the prognosis extremely poor even without melanoma. Read that against the phase above: the figure is conditioned on becoming symptomatic, and what proportion of those with the lesion become symptomatic is not established by any cited source.
Show evidence (4 references)
ORPHA:2481 SUPPORT DIRECT Other
"HP:0001522 | Death in infancy | Occasional (29-5%)"
Orphanet classes death in infancy as occasional. Recorded on this phase because HP:0001522 is a clinical-course term, outside the phenotypic-abnormality branch that the phenotype rows bind. The record states no population or denominator for the class.
PMID:35535823 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"NCM most often presents in pediatric patients within the first 2 years of life and is associated with high mortality due to proliferation of melanocytes in the brain. Prognosis is poor, as patients typically die within 3 years of symptom onset."
The timing and the outcome of this phase, from a systematic review of 74 published patients. Published cases over-represent the severe, so this is the prognosis of reported symptomatic disease rather than of the disease.
PMID:1869648 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Most patients with neurocutaneous melanosis presented in the first 2 years of life with neurologic manifestations of increased intracranial pressure, mass lesions, or spinal cord compression."
How this phase presents, in 39 reviewed cases: raised pressure, mass lesions or spinal cord compression, mostly in the first two years.
+ 1 more reference
Malignant transformation
Melanoma in the leptomeninges or the skin. For melanoma of the central nervous system in children with this disease, a German registry study with published cases added reports a median survival of 0.4 years from diagnosis; in 27 pooled cases of primary diffuse leptomeningeal melanomatosis, a related cancer, median overall survival is four months from diagnosis.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:33921303 SUPPORT INDIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Mortality remains substantial despite diverse therapeutic approaches with a median overall survival of 4 months from diagnosis."
Survival in 27 pooled cases of primary diffuse leptomeningeal melanomatosis, a cancer the review calls biologically closely related to this disease; not a survival figure for melanoma arising in this disease, and indirect for that reason.
PMID:38225622 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"In the expanded cohort, no patient survived longer than 1 year after diagnosis despite multimodal therapy (including trametinib; n = 9), with a median survival of 0.4 years (range 0.1-0.9)."
A survival figure for melanoma of the central nervous system in this disease: 27 children from a German registry and published reports, none of whom survived a year. The published cases may include children counted in other series in this entry.
📊

Prevalence

5
Japanese infants under one year treated for congenital melanocytic naevi, 2020-2022
Point prevalence 31600.0 per 100,000 >1 in 1,000
Not a population prevalence, and the denominator is what makes it meaningful: 38 infants starting treatment for congenital melanocytic naevi, all of whom were imaged. The quantity is the proportion of that group with the lesion on brain MRI, symptomatic or not, which is why the class reads as common and would be absurd read as a figure for the general population. The other proportions in this section measure other quantities in other groups, so they are recorded side by side rather than averaged.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37777590 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"NCM was diagnosed on brain MRI in 31.6% of the 38 patients with CMN and in 25.0% of patients with no prior examination or treatment."
The numerator and denominator together. Thirty-eight infants is a small denominator and the entry does not present this as a rate for the disease.
Patients with large congenital melanocytic naevi, internet-based registry
Point prevalence 4800.0 per 100,000 >1 in 1,000
A different quantity from the row above: this counts asymptomatic central nervous system involvement only - 4.8% of the 186 registry patients who had magnetic resonance imaging - where the row above counts the lesion on brain MRI whether or not there are symptoms. That difference does not close the gap: the same registry puts involvement of any kind at 26 of 379. This study is also self-reported, which its own limitations section states, while the row above is prospective imaging of every infant enrolled.

The figure and its denominator. Self-reported through a questionnaire, which the authors state as the method.
PMID:16310055 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical

The authors' own limitation, which is the main reason this figure and the prospective one are not averaged.
Children with multiple congenital melanocytic naevi, screened cohort, 1991-2013
Point prevalence 21000.0 per 100,000 >1 in 1,000
21% of the children with multiple congenital melanocytic naevi who had a screening scan in a specialist cohort; 289 met the criterion for one, and the abstract does not say how many were scanned. The denominator is children with more than one congenital melanocytic naevus at birth, not the population, and the numerator is an abnormal scan of any kind rather than melanocytosis specifically.

The figure and its denominator. Abnormal magnetic resonance imaging rather than melanocytosis, which is why this record and the 31.6% one are not the same quantity either.
Individuals with large congenital melanocytic naevi
Lifetime prevalence 12000.0 per 100,000 >1 in 1,000
About 12%, from a review, and the only one of this entry's conditional figures stated as a lifetime risk rather than a cross-sectional proportion. The same sentence names what raises it: wide extension and dorsal axial distribution of the naevus.

The figure and the features that raise it, from a review of the disease.
Europe, general population
Point prevalence 5.0 per 100,000 (1.0–9.0) 1–9 per 100,000
The only population figure in this entry, and the one a reader filtering on prevalence_class needs: every other record here is a proportion among people who already have the cutaneous disease, and those read as common because their denominator is small. Orphanet gives a band rather than a point estimate: the band itself is carried as the low and high rates, and the midpoint is recorded as the point value.

Differential Diagnoses

4

Conditions with similar clinical presentations that must be differentiated from Neurocutaneous Melanocytosis:

Melanoma metastatic to the leptomeninges Not Yet Curated MONDO:0005191
Overlapping Features Melanoma that has spread to the leptomeninges from a primary cutaneous tumour. A series of primary leptomeningeal melanocytic neoplasms names it as the differential diagnosis its genetic findings may help with, for primary tumours like those it studied. The 1991 diagnostic criteria of this disease exclude a patient whose skin and meningeal lesions are both malignant: they allow a cutaneous melanoma only where the meningeal lesions are histologically benign, and a meningeal melanoma only where the cutaneous lesions are benign.
Distinguishing Features
  • a cutaneous melanoma together with malignant meningeal lesions
  • meningeal melanoma counts towards this disease only where the cutaneous lesions are benign
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:41474606 SUPPORT INDIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"(3) no evidence of cutaneous melanoma, except in cases where meningeal lesions are histologically benign, (4) no evidence of meningeal melanoma, except in cases where the cutaneous lesions are benign."
The two exclusion criteria of 1991, as a 2026 case report's review restates them: within this disease the skin and meningeal lesions are not both malignant. Indirect for this row, because the criteria do not name metastasis.
PMID:25315378 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"This finding may help in the differential diagnosis between a primary LMN and a metastasis from a cutaneous melanoma to the central nervous system."
The series' authors offer their genetic finding as an aid in telling a primary leptomeningeal melanocytic neoplasm from a metastasis of cutaneous melanoma. Indirect for this disease, which the abstract does not mention.
Meningeal melanocytoma MONDO:0016746
Overlapping Features A primary melanocytic tumour of the meninges. In a series of 20 primary leptomeningeal melanocytic neoplasms, from melanocytomas to melanomas, most carried a GNAQ or GNA11 mutation and none an NRAS, BRAF or HRAS hotspot mutation. A later series from the same group sets this disease against them: in it, large or multiple congenital naevi accompany the leptomeningeal tumour, and the same NRAS mutation is frequently found in both.
Distinguishing Features
  • no congenital melanocytic naevi
  • GNAQ or GNA11 mutation in most of a mixed series, and no NRAS, BRAF or HRAS hotspot mutation
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:25315378 SUPPORT INDIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human clinical
"GNAQ(Q209) mutations were present in eleven LMNs, while two of 20 cases carried a GNA11(Q209) mutation. No BRAF, HRAS or NRAS hotspot mutations were detected."
A GNAQ mutation in 11 and a GNA11 mutation in 2 of 20 primary leptomeningeal melanocytic neoplasms, and an NRAS hotspot mutation in none. Indirect: the series reports melanocytomas and melanomas together, and its abstract does not mention this disease.
PMID:26769193 SUPPORT DIRECT BACKGROUND Human clinical
"Patients with neurocutaneous melanocytosis have a large and/or multiple congenital melanocytic nevi of the skin in association with a primary LMN ... Instead of GNAQ or GNA11 mutations, these patients frequently demonstrate identical NRAS mutations in both the congenital melanocytic nevus and in the LMN"
How this disease differs, from the later series' discussion, citing earlier work: large or multiple congenital naevi with the leptomeningeal tumour, and frequently the same NRAS mutation in both, instead of a GNAQ or GNA11 mutation.
Primary leptomeningeal melanoma without congenital naevi Not Yet Curated MONDO:0003761
Overlapping Features Primary melanoma of the leptomeninges in a patient without congenital naevi. The 2015 series of primary leptomeningeal melanocytic neoplasms, which includes melanomas, found a GNAQ or GNA11 mutation in most and no NRAS hotspot mutation; the later series from the same group, whose patients were all adults, sets this disease against them for its frequent NRAS mutation. The exception to watch is a child: in the trametinib series, whose four children all had NRAS-mutated melanoma, a child without skin lesions had a leptomeningeal melanoma indistinguishable from those of the children with naevi, which its authors suggest could be the same post-zygotic mutation affecting only the nervous system.
Distinguishing Features
  • no congenital melanocytic naevi
  • in adult mixed series, usually a GNAQ or GNA11 mutation rather than an NRAS one

The same series' result, which covers melanocytomas and melanomas together, so it does not give the figure for melanomas alone; its abstract gives no ages.

+ 2 more references
Other mosaic RASopathies Not Yet Curated MONDO:0021060
Overlapping Features Mosaic conditions that a review of the RASopathies lists with this disease: sebaceous naevus syndrome, McCune-Albright syndrome, phakomatosis pigmentokeratotica, epidermal naevus syndrome and encephalocraniocutaneous lipomatosis. The review's abstract does not compare their presentation with that of this disease. It notes that many RASopathies can be diagnosed clinically from their cutaneous findings; the skin lesion of this disease is the congenital melanocytic naevus.
Distinguishing Features
  • the cutaneous findings, from which the review says many RASopathies can be diagnosed clinically
Show evidence (2 references)

The review's list of mosaic RASopathies, with this disease among them. Indirect: listing the conditions together does not say that they resemble this disease.
PMID:40532825 SUPPORT INDIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human clinical
"Many RASopathies can be clinically diagnosed based on their cutaneous findings, thus it is essential for dermatologists to be comfortable differentiating RASopathies for accurate diagnosis and appropriate management."
The review says many RASopathies can be diagnosed clinically from their cutaneous findings; its abstract does not say how each is told apart from this disease.

Clinical Trials

3
NCT07891286 PHASE_II NOT_RECRUITING
A single-group phase II study of the MEK inhibitor mirdametinib, taken by mouth in four-week cycles for up to two years, in people aged 3 to 40 with large or giant congenital melanocytic naevi that carry a somatic NRAS or BRAF variant or one of the gene fusions the record lists. Its primary endpoint is an objective decrease in the largest diameter of the naevus. The registry lists neurocutaneous melanocytosis among its conditions and admits people with it only if it is asymptomatic and not progressing, and participants have MRI of the brain and spinal cord. Registered by the US National Cancer Institute as not yet recruiting, with an estimated start in January 2027 and an estimated enrolment of 15; NOT_RECRUITING is the closest status the schema has.
Target Phenotypes: large or giant congenital melanocytic naevi HP:0000995 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP) Relation: this clinical trial targets this phenotype This clinical trial targets large or giant congenital melanocytic naevi, annotated with Melanocytic nevus (HP:0000995). HP:0000995 is a phenotype from the Human Phenotype Ontology.
Show evidence (2 references)
clinicaltrials:NCT07891286 SUPPORT Other
"Researchers want to find out if a drug approved for a different use (mirdametinib) can shrink the size of LGCMN."
The trial's aim as the registry states it: a MEK inhibitor tested against the naevus itself.
clinicaltrials:NCT07891286 SUPPORT Other
"Magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan: They will lie on a bed that slides into a tube; the scan will use large magnets to create images of their brain and spinal cord."
Participants are imaged at the brain and spinal cord, where this disease's neural lesion lies.
NCT04548817 NOT_APPLICABLE RECRUITING
An observational registry, run from Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center, that collects retrospective and prospective clinical, treatment and outcome data on patients with large cutaneous melanocytic naevi present at birth or neurocutaneous melanocytosis, including patients with central nervous system melanocytosis and no skin lesion. Recruiting since September 2020, with an estimated enrolment of 100.
Show evidence (1 reference)
clinicaltrials:NCT04548817 SUPPORT Other
"This study will involve collecting information about the regular medical care you receive for large cutaneous melanocytic nevi (LCMN) or neurocutaneous melanocytosis (NCM)."
What the registry collects: the routine care these patients receive, with no intervention of its own.
NCT06828822 NOT_APPLICABLE RECRUITING
A prospective cohort, run from Nantes University Hospital, of children under 2 years with medium, large or giant congenital naevi, whose primary outcome is the prevalence of neurodevelopmental abnormalities at 3 years by naevus size and number. The record names neuro-meningeal melanosis among the neurological risks and asks whether routine brain imaging is needed. Registered as interventional with phase not applicable, its interventions being the study's own assessments (a neurodevelopmental assessment, a quality-of-life measure and a meeting with the parents); recruiting since July 2025, with an estimated enrolment of 819.
Target Phenotypes: neurodevelopmental abnormality HP:0012759 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP) Relation: this clinical trial targets this phenotype This clinical trial targets neurodevelopmental abnormality (HP:0012759). HP:0012759 is a phenotype from the Human Phenotype Ontology.
Show evidence (2 references)
clinicaltrials:NCT06828822 SUPPORT Other
"Large CN can be associated with neurological abnormalities such as neuro-meningeal melanosis, hydrocephalus, or brain malformations."
Why the cohort follows these children: the record names neuro-meningeal melanosis among the neurological risks of large naevi.
clinicaltrials:NCT06828822 SUPPORT Other
"There is an urgent need for further research to clarify best practices in monitoring and treatment, including the need for routine brain imaging and criteria for surgical intervention."
The screening question the cohort asks, which the entry's discussion on screening also records.

Experimental Models

1
Patient-derived nevomelanocytic spheroids PRIMARY_CELL_CULTURE
Spheroids grown from spinal cord, brain and skin lesions of four patients, carrying the same NRAS mutation as the lesions they came from. It is one of two patient-derived cell systems in the cited literature and the only one in which several inhibitors are compared against each other; the other, from a leptomeningeal melanomatosis report, is described only as giving first insights into in vitro drug sensitivities.
Pathograph links
Mosaic Melanocytic Clone Spanning Skin and Neuraxis measures fidelity: moderate observes: cellular The cells are the clone, taken from both compartments of the same patients, and their identity was confirmed by the mutation rather than assumed.
Limitations: Cells out of their tissue: the leptomeningeal environment, the cerebrospinal fluid compartment and the skin's architecture are all absent, and clonogenic growth in Geltrex is a proxy for what the lesion does in a patient.
Divergences from the human mechanism
boundary omission qualifying
Neither the leptomeningeal compartment nor the dermal environment is in the model, so nothing it shows bears on why the lesion sits where it does or why it obstructs cerebrospinal fluid.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:26354928 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In vitro / ex vivo
"We generated nevomelanocytic spheroids in vitro from lesions of the spinal cord, brain, and skin from 4 NCM patients."
What the system is and where it came from: three compartments, four patients.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:26354928 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In vitro / ex vivo
"Skin-derived cells formed more colonies than CNS-derived cells."
A difference between the two compartments of the same clone, which is a result this system can give and a patient cannot.
🐁

Animal Models

1
Embryonic melanocyte NRAS(G12D) mouse
Oncogenic NRAS driven in melanocytes during embryogenesis. It produces the congenital melanocytic lesions and the central nervous system melanoma of this disease, and it does not produce cutaneous melanoma.
Species
Mouse
Genotype
oncogenic NRAS (G12D) expressed in melanocytes of developing embryos
Publication
PMID:23303902
Pathograph links
Mosaic Melanocytic Clone Spanning Skin and Neuraxis partially recapitulates fidelity: moderate observes: cellular Reproduces the embryonic timing and the two-compartment distribution: congenital melanocytic lesions in the skin and melanocytic disease in the central nervous system from one driver.
Limitations: The allele is G12D. Every mutation-positive patient in the series that established the lesion carries a codon 61 substitution instead - twelve of fifteen patients, the other three being mutation-negative - and a further cited cohort finds BRAF V600E rather than NRAS in two of sixteen, and the mouse's cutaneous lesions are described as resembling blue naevi rather than congenital melanocytic naevi. The mouse is a conditional transgenic rather than a mosaic, so the one feature that defines the human disease - mutant and wild-type cells side by side from a post-zygotic event - is not what the model has.
Divergences from the human mechanism
proxy quantity qualifying
The model's quantity is NRAS G12D expressed from a transgene in a melanocyte lineage. The node's quantity is a codon 61 substitution present post-zygotically in one clone among wild-type cells.
species mismatch qualifying
The cutaneous lesion the mouse makes is reported as resembling human blue naevus, which is not the congenital melanocytic naevus this node's descendants form in patients.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:23303902 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"When NRAS(G12D) was expressed in the melanocytes of developing embryos, it induced melanocyte proliferation and congenital melanocytic lesions reminiscent of human blue nevi but did not induce cutaneous melanoma."
What the model does and does not reproduce, in the authors' own words, which is also the source of both divergences recorded above.
Malignant Transformation to Leptomeningeal Melanoma partially recapitulates fidelity: moderate observes: organism The model's unexpected result and the reason it is cited here: early-onset primary melanoma of the central nervous system, which is the human disease's lethal arm.
Limitations: The human tumours are leptomeningeal and arise within a pre-existing melanocytosis; the mouse tumours are described as primary central nervous system melanoma without that substrate being characterised in the cached abstract.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:23303902 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Model organism
"Unexpectedly, however, it did induce early-onset primary melanoma of the central nervous system (CNS)."
The arm the model does reproduce.
No MGI identifier is bound for the targeted gene. The construct is human-numbered oncogenic NRAS expressed in mouse melanocytes and the cached abstract names no mouse allele, so the gene is left in the genotype string rather than bound to a model-organism identifier that would have to be written from memory.

name: Neurocutaneous Melanocytosis
creation_date: '2026-11-13T20:10:00Z'
disease_term:
  preferred_term: neurocutaneous melanocytosis
  term:
    id: MONDO:0009578
    label: neurocutaneous melanocytosis
categories:
- Neurocutaneous disorder
- Mosaic disorder
- Neurological disorder
parents:
- congenital melanocytic naevus syndrome
synonyms:
- NCM
- neurocutaneous melanosis
- melanosis neurocutanea
description: >-
  A mosaic disorder in which one post-zygotic NRAS mutation, arising in a melanoblast precursor before
  the neural crest has finished migrating, leaves melanocytic lesions in two places at once: congenital
  melanocytic naevi in the skin, and melanocytosis of the leptomeninges and brain parenchyma. The same
  codon 61 mutation is found in the skin and in the central nervous system lesions of one patient and is
  absent from their blood, which is what makes this one disease rather than two. Most central nervous
  system involvement is benign and much of it is silent, but it can obstruct cerebrospinal fluid flow,
  cause epilepsy, and transform to melanoma; symptomatic disease carries a poor prognosis. Non-melanocytic malformations - Dandy-Walker, arachnoid cysts, cortical dysplasia - occur in the
  same patients, and the non-melanocytic central nervous system samples that were sequenced in one
  series all carried the mutation. That series does not say which lesions those samples came from,
  so the mutation is established for the class and not for any named malformation.

    hypertrichosis and with smaller satellite naevi. Their size, distribution, satellite count and
    surface features are what predict the neural half, which is why they are a mechanism node here and
    not only a phenotype.

- name: Leptomeningeal and Parenchymal Melanocytosis
  role: effector
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  description: >-
    Melanocytes where there should be almost none: infiltrating the leptomeninges and deposited in
    the brain parenchyma. In a screening series of 38 Japanese infants with congenital melanocytic
    naevi, about a third had it on brain MRI; that series' abstract gives no symptom data, and no
    cited source, as cached, establishes what proportion of those found on screening go on to
    neurological disease. It is the lesion every consequence below depends on.
  locations:
  - preferred_term: leptomeninx
    term:
      id: UBERON:0000391
      label: leptomeninx
  - preferred_term: amygdala
    term:
      id: UBERON:0001876
      label: amygdala
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: melanocyte
    term:
      id: CL:0000148
      label: melanocyte
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:37777590
    reference_title: >-
      Incidence of neurocutaneous melanosis in Japanese pediatric patients with congenital
      melanocytic nevi.
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      NCM was diagnosed on brain MRI in 31.6% of the 38 patients with CMN and in 25.0% of patients
      with no prior examination or treatment.
    explanation: >-
      How common the lesion was among these infants, all of whom were imaged: about a third. Read
      the denominator - 38 Japanese infants starting treatment for their naevi, not an unselected
      population.
  - reference: PMID:22469364
    reference_title: >-
      Spectrum of central nervous system abnormalities in neurocutaneous melanocytosis.
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      All patients had neuroimaging: six had findings suggestive of diffuse leptomeningeal
      melanocytosis; seven had multifocal melanocytic deposits; and one patient had normal
      neuroimaging but focal seizures.
    explanation: >-
      The two radiological forms this node covers, in fourteen children referred to a neuro-oncology
      service - and the patient with seizures and a normal scan, which is why imaging is not the
      definition of the lesion.
  notes: >-
    The denominators on this node point in opposite directions and both are needed. A screening
    series of infants with congenital melanocytic naevi finds the lesion in about a third of them,
    and its abstract does not say how many had symptoms; a neuro-oncology referral series finds
    diffuse leptomeningeal disease in six of fourteen and four leptomeningeal melanomas among six
    deaths. Neither is the disease's natural history: one counts who has the lesion and the other
    counts who was referred to a neuro-oncology service.

      measures the obstruction; what they report is hydrocephalus in patients with the lesion, which is
      why the edge is drawn through unknown intermediates.

  - target: Cranial nerve palsy
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Melanocytic infiltration of the leptomeninges is where the cranial nerves run, and cranial

      intermediates and could as well run through the raised pressure node.

  - target: Global developmental delay
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Profound delay occurs in a minority of children with the lesion and most have none or only mild
      delay, so this edge asserts a possible consequence and not a usual one. What separates them is
      partly established: in the largest screened cohort an abnormal scan was the most significant
      predictor of abnormal neurodevelopment, and the cohort split into intraparenchymal melanosis
      alone, which was not associated with malignancy or death even when symptomatic, and all other
      pathology, which was not so benign.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:22469364
      reference_title: >-
        Spectrum of central nervous system abnormalities in neurocutaneous melanocytosis.

        delay.
      explanation: >-
        Both halves, because three of fourteen is what the edge claims and eleven of fourteen is
        what keeps it from being read as the rule. INDIRECT: an outcome counted in children with
        the lesion, not derived from it.
  - target: Seizures
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Epilepsy is the commonest neurological manifestation, and in the pooled neurosurgical literature
      the commonest symptom of any kind. How the melanocytic deposits produce it is not established by
      any cited source; focal cortical dysplasia in the same patients is one route and the deposits
      themselves another.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:35535823
      reference_title: >-
        Neurosurgical management of patients with neurocutaneous melanosis: a systematic review.
      supports: SUPPORT
      directness: INDIRECT
      quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: >-
        Seizures were the most frequently reported symptom (55%, 41/74 cases).
      explanation: >-
        The frequency, pooled across 74 published patients. INDIRECT for the edge: a symptom
        frequency in patients with the lesion is not a demonstration that the lesion produced it,
        and a pooled case-report series over-represents the symptomatic.
  - target: Malignant Transformation to Leptomeningeal Melanoma
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES

      The melanocytosis is the substrate melanoma arises in. A second genetic event is implicated rather

- name: Malignant Transformation to Leptomeningeal Melanoma
  role: effector
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >-
    Melanoma arising within the leptomeningeal lesion. In a referral series of 14 patients, four of
    the six who died had leptomeningeal melanoma. In children with melanoma of the central nervous
    system in this disease, a German registry study with published cases added reports a median
    survival of 0.4 years; in 27 pooled cases of primary diffuse leptomeningeal melanomatosis, a
    related cancer, median overall survival is four months from diagnosis.

- name: Non-Melanocytic Central Nervous System Malformation

    why they are in the chain.

    supports: SUPPORT

    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-

      unrecognized increased intracranial pressure (present in 67%), is a frequent reason for the

    explanation: >-

- category: Neoplasm
  name: Melanoma

    preferred_term: melanoma arising in the central nervous system or the skin
    term:
      id: HP:0002861
      label: Melanoma
  description: >-
    The malignant outcome. Every death in the 14-patient referral series had the leptomeningeal
    lesion and two thirds of those deaths had melanoma in it; the cited sources, as cached, do not
    count cutaneous melanoma in the same children, so the entry does not compare the two sites.
  evidence:

    reference_title: Neurocutaneous melanocytosis
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: HP:0002861 | Melanoma | Occasional (29-5%)
    explanation: >-
      Orphanet's frequency class for melanoma, occasional, without saying whether it counts the
      leptomeningeal or the cutaneous tumour, and with no stated population or denominator. No band
      is set: the literature review cited on the Symptomatic neurological disease phase found
      leptomeningeal melanoma in 62% of 39 published cases, and the two would put the finding in
      different bands.

    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
